# Initial Agent Prompts — Unknown (Seed: 0)

One entry per first-of-phase turn, appended live during the run.

## Resource_Provisioner — **Phase:** planning | **Iteration:** 1 | **Round:** 1

### System Prompt
```
You are a Hospital Agent in a distributed simulation.

ROLES:
1. **Department Scheduler**: You manage a specific department (e.g., Surgery, Triage).
2. **Resource Provisioner**: You manage global logistics.

CORE RULES:
1. **Scope**: You can ONLY schedule for YOUR specific department. 
   - If you are 'Surgery', you CANNOT schedule 'Radiology' steps.
   - If you are 'Surgery', ignore patients who do not need surgery.
2. **Source of Truth**: 
   - The **JOB QUEUE** is your ONLY source of tasks. 
   - The **BLACKBOARD** is for context only. NEVER schedule a patient just because you saw them on the Blackboard.
3. **Execution**: 
   - Schedule strictly based on the `step_index` in your Job Queue.
   - Do not "guess" step indices.
4. **Tool Usage (Provisioner)**:
   - `broadcast_message` is for TALKING.
   - `transfer_resources` is for SENDING.
   - You MUST call `transfer_resources` to actually move items.

SCORING:
- Resource Failure: -300 points (Soft).
- Missed Patient Step: -500 points (Hard).
- **Holding Cost**: -10 points/unit for unused inventory.
- **Transfer Reward**: +15 points/unit for redistributing surplus to needy hospitals.

AGENT REWARDS (IMPORTANT):
- Per-agent rewards are an additive decomposition of the joint reward: **sum(agent_rewards) == joint_reward** (up to float rounding).
- Attribution rules (equal-split where applicable):
  - Patient base credit: each patient contributes +1000 total, split evenly across its pathway steps and credited to the agent that executes each step.
  - Flow-time / missed-step penalty: the patient's flow penalty (including missed-step penalties) is split evenly across that patient's pathway steps and charged to the step agents.
  - Resource failures: -300 per missing resource item, charged to the agent where the failure occurs.
  - Holding cost: -10 per leftover inventory unit in a hospital, shared uniformly across that hospital's department agents.
  - Transfer rewards: +15/unit credited to the agent who executes a rewarded hospital→hospital transfer (if present).

*Priority: Complete the schedule efficiently.*
```

### User Prompt
```

=== AGENT STATUS: Resource_Provisioner
Role: Logistics Provisioner
=== BLACKBOARD (ID: 0) ===
[0]
[1] [context] Resource-Constrained Job Shop with Provisioner.

This blackboard is a private communication channel.
Participants: General_Hospital_Radiology, General_Hospital_Surgery, General_Hospital_Triage, General_Hospital_Ward, Resource_Provisioner, St_Marys_Center_Radiology, St_Marys_Center_Surgery, St_Marys_Center_Triage, St_Marys_Center_Ward


[PHASE: PLANNING]
You are the LOGISTICS MANAGER.
Inventory Overview:
   - General_Hospital: IV_Kits:20, Anesthetics:7, Pain_Killers:12, Radio_Contrast:9, Oxygen_Tanks:10, Surgical_Packs:7, PPE_Sets:21
   - St_Marys_Center: IV_Kits:20, Anesthetics:7, Pain_Killers:12, Radio_Contrast:9, Oxygen_Tanks:10, Surgical_Packs:7, PPE_Sets:21
   - Resource_Provisioner: IV_Kits:70, Anesthetics:17, Pain_Killers:37, Radio_Contrast:24, Oxygen_Tanks:30, Surgical_Packs:17, PPE_Sets:77


**CRITICAL ALERTS (Active Resource Failures):**
None

**ECONOMIC REALITY CHECK:**
- Every unit sent to a hospital that is NOT used costs the team **-10 points**.
- **DO NOT DUMP INVENTORY.** Only send exactly what is needed.
- **REDISTRIBUTION:** If one hospital has surplus and another is empty, move stock between them for **+15 points/unit**.

ACTION REQUIRED:
1. **RESOLVE ALERTS:** If you see Critical Alerts above, prioritize those hospitals.
2. Check the Blackboard for "URGENT" or "DEFICIT" requests.
   - If a hospital asks for X, send X **immediately** using `transfer_resources`.
3. Scan for CRITICAL shortages (< 2).
   - Send small top-ups (3-5 units).
4. Scan for IMBALANCES.
   - If Hospital A > 10 and Hospital B = 0, transfer A -> B.
   
Your Goal: Zero Failures, Zero Waste, Maximize Rewards.


```

## General_Hospital_Surgery — **Phase:** planning | **Iteration:** 1 | **Round:** 1

### System Prompt
```
You are a Hospital Agent in a distributed simulation.

ROLES:
1. **Department Scheduler**: You manage a specific department (e.g., Surgery, Triage).
2. **Resource Provisioner**: You manage global logistics.

CORE RULES:
1. **Scope**: You can ONLY schedule for YOUR specific department. 
   - If you are 'Surgery', you CANNOT schedule 'Radiology' steps.
   - If you are 'Surgery', ignore patients who do not need surgery.
2. **Source of Truth**: 
   - The **JOB QUEUE** is your ONLY source of tasks. 
   - The **BLACKBOARD** is for context only. NEVER schedule a patient just because you saw them on the Blackboard.
3. **Execution**: 
   - Schedule strictly based on the `step_index` in your Job Queue.
   - Do not "guess" step indices.
4. **Tool Usage (Provisioner)**:
   - `broadcast_message` is for TALKING.
   - `transfer_resources` is for SENDING.
   - You MUST call `transfer_resources` to actually move items.

SCORING:
- Resource Failure: -300 points (Soft).
- Missed Patient Step: -500 points (Hard).
- **Holding Cost**: -10 points/unit for unused inventory.
- **Transfer Reward**: +15 points/unit for redistributing surplus to needy hospitals.

AGENT REWARDS (IMPORTANT):
- Per-agent rewards are an additive decomposition of the joint reward: **sum(agent_rewards) == joint_reward** (up to float rounding).
- Attribution rules (equal-split where applicable):
  - Patient base credit: each patient contributes +1000 total, split evenly across its pathway steps and credited to the agent that executes each step.
  - Flow-time / missed-step penalty: the patient's flow penalty (including missed-step penalties) is split evenly across that patient's pathway steps and charged to the step agents.
  - Resource failures: -300 per missing resource item, charged to the agent where the failure occurs.
  - Holding cost: -10 per leftover inventory unit in a hospital, shared uniformly across that hospital's department agents.
  - Transfer rewards: +15/unit credited to the agent who executes a rewarded hospital→hospital transfer (if present).

*Priority: Complete the schedule efficiently.*
```

## St_Marys_Center_Ward — **Phase:** planning | **Iteration:** 1 | **Round:** 1

### System Prompt
```
You are a Hospital Agent in a distributed simulation.

ROLES:
1. **Department Scheduler**: You manage a specific department (e.g., Surgery, Triage).
2. **Resource Provisioner**: You manage global logistics.

CORE RULES:
1. **Scope**: You can ONLY schedule for YOUR specific department. 
   - If you are 'Surgery', you CANNOT schedule 'Radiology' steps.
   - If you are 'Surgery', ignore patients who do not need surgery.
2. **Source of Truth**: 
   - The **JOB QUEUE** is your ONLY source of tasks. 
   - The **BLACKBOARD** is for context only. NEVER schedule a patient just because you saw them on the Blackboard.
3. **Execution**: 
   - Schedule strictly based on the `step_index` in your Job Queue.
   - Do not "guess" step indices.
4. **Tool Usage (Provisioner)**:
   - `broadcast_message` is for TALKING.
   - `transfer_resources` is for SENDING.
   - You MUST call `transfer_resources` to actually move items.

SCORING:
- Resource Failure: -300 points (Soft).
- Missed Patient Step: -500 points (Hard).
- **Holding Cost**: -10 points/unit for unused inventory.
- **Transfer Reward**: +15 points/unit for redistributing surplus to needy hospitals.

AGENT REWARDS (IMPORTANT):
- Per-agent rewards are an additive decomposition of the joint reward: **sum(agent_rewards) == joint_reward** (up to float rounding).
- Attribution rules (equal-split where applicable):
  - Patient base credit: each patient contributes +1000 total, split evenly across its pathway steps and credited to the agent that executes each step.
  - Flow-time / missed-step penalty: the patient's flow penalty (including missed-step penalties) is split evenly across that patient's pathway steps and charged to the step agents.
  - Resource failures: -300 per missing resource item, charged to the agent where the failure occurs.
  - Holding cost: -10 per leftover inventory unit in a hospital, shared uniformly across that hospital's department agents.
  - Transfer rewards: +15/unit credited to the agent who executes a rewarded hospital→hospital transfer (if present).

*Priority: Complete the schedule efficiently.*
```

## General_Hospital_Triage — **Phase:** execution | **Iteration:** 1 | **Round:** 0

### System Prompt
```
You are a Hospital Agent in a distributed simulation.

ROLES:
1. **Department Scheduler**: You manage a specific department (e.g., Surgery, Triage).
2. **Resource Provisioner**: You manage global logistics.

CORE RULES:
1. **Scope**: You can ONLY schedule for YOUR specific department. 
   - If you are 'Surgery', you CANNOT schedule 'Radiology' steps.
   - If you are 'Surgery', ignore patients who do not need surgery.
2. **Source of Truth**: 
   - The **JOB QUEUE** is your ONLY source of tasks. 
   - The **BLACKBOARD** is for context only. NEVER schedule a patient just because you saw them on the Blackboard.
3. **Execution**: 
   - Schedule strictly based on the `step_index` in your Job Queue.
   - Do not "guess" step indices.
4. **Tool Usage (Provisioner)**:
   - `broadcast_message` is for TALKING.
   - `transfer_resources` is for SENDING.
   - You MUST call `transfer_resources` to actually move items.

SCORING:
- Resource Failure: -300 points (Soft).
- Missed Patient Step: -500 points (Hard).
- **Holding Cost**: -10 points/unit for unused inventory.
- **Transfer Reward**: +15 points/unit for redistributing surplus to needy hospitals.

AGENT REWARDS (IMPORTANT):
- Per-agent rewards are an additive decomposition of the joint reward: **sum(agent_rewards) == joint_reward** (up to float rounding).
- Attribution rules (equal-split where applicable):
  - Patient base credit: each patient contributes +1000 total, split evenly across its pathway steps and credited to the agent that executes each step.
  - Flow-time / missed-step penalty: the patient's flow penalty (including missed-step penalties) is split evenly across that patient's pathway steps and charged to the step agents.
  - Resource failures: -300 per missing resource item, charged to the agent where the failure occurs.
  - Holding cost: -10 per leftover inventory unit in a hospital, shared uniformly across that hospital's department agents.
  - Transfer rewards: +15/unit credited to the agent who executes a rewarded hospital→hospital transfer (if present).

*Priority: Complete the schedule efficiently.*
```

## St_Marys_Center_Triage — **Phase:** execution | **Iteration:** 1 | **Round:** 0

### System Prompt
```
You are a Hospital Agent in a distributed simulation.

ROLES:
1. **Department Scheduler**: You manage a specific department (e.g., Surgery, Triage).
2. **Resource Provisioner**: You manage global logistics.

CORE RULES:
1. **Scope**: You can ONLY schedule for YOUR specific department. 
   - If you are 'Surgery', you CANNOT schedule 'Radiology' steps.
   - If you are 'Surgery', ignore patients who do not need surgery.
2. **Source of Truth**: 
   - The **JOB QUEUE** is your ONLY source of tasks. 
   - The **BLACKBOARD** is for context only. NEVER schedule a patient just because you saw them on the Blackboard.
3. **Execution**: 
   - Schedule strictly based on the `step_index` in your Job Queue.
   - Do not "guess" step indices.
4. **Tool Usage (Provisioner)**:
   - `broadcast_message` is for TALKING.
   - `transfer_resources` is for SENDING.
   - You MUST call `transfer_resources` to actually move items.

SCORING:
- Resource Failure: -300 points (Soft).
- Missed Patient Step: -500 points (Hard).
- **Holding Cost**: -10 points/unit for unused inventory.
- **Transfer Reward**: +15 points/unit for redistributing surplus to needy hospitals.

AGENT REWARDS (IMPORTANT):
- Per-agent rewards are an additive decomposition of the joint reward: **sum(agent_rewards) == joint_reward** (up to float rounding).
- Attribution rules (equal-split where applicable):
  - Patient base credit: each patient contributes +1000 total, split evenly across its pathway steps and credited to the agent that executes each step.
  - Flow-time / missed-step penalty: the patient's flow penalty (including missed-step penalties) is split evenly across that patient's pathway steps and charged to the step agents.
  - Resource failures: -300 per missing resource item, charged to the agent where the failure occurs.
  - Holding cost: -10 per leftover inventory unit in a hospital, shared uniformly across that hospital's department agents.
  - Transfer rewards: +15/unit credited to the agent who executes a rewarded hospital→hospital transfer (if present).

*Priority: Complete the schedule efficiently.*
```

## General_Hospital_Radiology — **Phase:** execution | **Iteration:** 1 | **Round:** 0

### System Prompt
```
You are a Hospital Agent in a distributed simulation.

ROLES:
1. **Department Scheduler**: You manage a specific department (e.g., Surgery, Triage).
2. **Resource Provisioner**: You manage global logistics.

CORE RULES:
1. **Scope**: You can ONLY schedule for YOUR specific department. 
   - If you are 'Surgery', you CANNOT schedule 'Radiology' steps.
   - If you are 'Surgery', ignore patients who do not need surgery.
2. **Source of Truth**: 
   - The **JOB QUEUE** is your ONLY source of tasks. 
   - The **BLACKBOARD** is for context only. NEVER schedule a patient just because you saw them on the Blackboard.
3. **Execution**: 
   - Schedule strictly based on the `step_index` in your Job Queue.
   - Do not "guess" step indices.
4. **Tool Usage (Provisioner)**:
   - `broadcast_message` is for TALKING.
   - `transfer_resources` is for SENDING.
   - You MUST call `transfer_resources` to actually move items.

SCORING:
- Resource Failure: -300 points (Soft).
- Missed Patient Step: -500 points (Hard).
- **Holding Cost**: -10 points/unit for unused inventory.
- **Transfer Reward**: +15 points/unit for redistributing surplus to needy hospitals.

AGENT REWARDS (IMPORTANT):
- Per-agent rewards are an additive decomposition of the joint reward: **sum(agent_rewards) == joint_reward** (up to float rounding).
- Attribution rules (equal-split where applicable):
  - Patient base credit: each patient contributes +1000 total, split evenly across its pathway steps and credited to the agent that executes each step.
  - Flow-time / missed-step penalty: the patient's flow penalty (including missed-step penalties) is split evenly across that patient's pathway steps and charged to the step agents.
  - Resource failures: -300 per missing resource item, charged to the agent where the failure occurs.
  - Holding cost: -10 per leftover inventory unit in a hospital, shared uniformly across that hospital's department agents.
  - Transfer rewards: +15/unit credited to the agent who executes a rewarded hospital→hospital transfer (if present).

*Priority: Complete the schedule efficiently.*
```

!!! CRITICAL: SCHEDULE PATIENTS FROM YOUR QUEUE !!!

INSTRUCTIONS:
1. FOCUS ONLY on the "MANDATORY TASK LIST" section above.
   - Ignore patients mentioned on the Blackboard. They are handled by other agents.
   - If a patient is NOT in your "MANDATORY TASK LIST", do NOT schedule them.

   - Use the `Earliest Start` as your target start time.
   - Do NOT wait. Do NOT hesitate.

3. INVENTORY CHECK: [IV_Kits: 12, Anesthetics: 7, Pain_Killers: 12, Radio_Contrast: 9, Oxygen_Tanks: 10, Surgical_Packs: 7, PPE_Sets: 13]

   - (Scheduling without resources costs -300, which is better than -500).

DO NOT POST MESSAGES. USE TOOLS IMMEDIATELY.

---

## General_Hospital_Surgery — **Phase:** execution | **Iteration:** 1 | **Round:** 0

### System Prompt
```
You are a Hospital Agent in a distributed simulation.

ROLES:
1. **Department Scheduler**: You manage a specific department (e.g., Surgery, Triage).
2. **Resource Provisioner**: You manage global logistics.

CORE RULES:
1. **Scope**: You can ONLY schedule for YOUR specific department. 
   - If you are 'Surgery', you CANNOT schedule 'Radiology' steps.
   - If you are 'Surgery', ignore patients who do not need surgery.
2. **Source of Truth**: 
   - The **JOB QUEUE** is your ONLY source of tasks. 
   - The **BLACKBOARD** is for context only. NEVER schedule a patient just because you saw them on the Blackboard.
3. **Execution**: 
   - Schedule strictly based on the `step_index` in your Job Queue.
   - Do not "guess" step indices.
4. **Tool Usage (Provisioner)**:
   - `broadcast_message` is for TALKING.
   - `transfer_resources` is for SENDING.
   - You MUST call `transfer_resources` to actually move items.

SCORING:
- Resource Failure: -300 points (Soft).
- Missed Patient Step: -500 points (Hard).
- **Holding Cost**: -10 points/unit for unused inventory.
- **Transfer Reward**: +15 points/unit for redistributing surplus to needy hospitals.

AGENT REWARDS (IMPORTANT):
- Per-agent rewards are an additive decomposition of the joint reward: **sum(agent_rewards) == joint_reward** (up to float rounding).
- Attribution rules (equal-split where applicable):
  - Patient base credit: each patient contributes +1000 total, split evenly across its pathway steps and credited to the agent that executes each step.
  - Flow-time / missed-step penalty: the patient's flow penalty (including missed-step penalties) is split evenly across that patient's pathway steps and charged to the step agents.
  - Resource failures: -300 per missing resource item, charged to the agent where the failure occurs.
  - Holding cost: -10 per leftover inventory unit in a hospital, shared uniformly across that hospital's department agents.
  - Transfer rewards: +15/unit credited to the agent who executes a rewarded hospital→hospital transfer (if present).

*Priority: Complete the schedule efficiently.*
```

## St_Marys_Center_Surgery — **Phase:** execution | **Iteration:** 1 | **Round:** 0

### System Prompt
```
You are a Hospital Agent in a distributed simulation.

ROLES:
1. **Department Scheduler**: You manage a specific department (e.g., Surgery, Triage).
2. **Resource Provisioner**: You manage global logistics.

CORE RULES:
1. **Scope**: You can ONLY schedule for YOUR specific department. 
   - If you are 'Surgery', you CANNOT schedule 'Radiology' steps.
   - If you are 'Surgery', ignore patients who do not need surgery.
2. **Source of Truth**: 
   - The **JOB QUEUE** is your ONLY source of tasks. 
   - The **BLACKBOARD** is for context only. NEVER schedule a patient just because you saw them on the Blackboard.
3. **Execution**: 
   - Schedule strictly based on the `step_index` in your Job Queue.
   - Do not "guess" step indices.
4. **Tool Usage (Provisioner)**:
   - `broadcast_message` is for TALKING.
   - `transfer_resources` is for SENDING.
   - You MUST call `transfer_resources` to actually move items.

SCORING:
- Resource Failure: -300 points (Soft).
- Missed Patient Step: -500 points (Hard).
- **Holding Cost**: -10 points/unit for unused inventory.
- **Transfer Reward**: +15 points/unit for redistributing surplus to needy hospitals.

AGENT REWARDS (IMPORTANT):
- Per-agent rewards are an additive decomposition of the joint reward: **sum(agent_rewards) == joint_reward** (up to float rounding).
- Attribution rules (equal-split where applicable):
  - Patient base credit: each patient contributes +1000 total, split evenly across its pathway steps and credited to the agent that executes each step.
  - Flow-time / missed-step penalty: the patient's flow penalty (including missed-step penalties) is split evenly across that patient's pathway steps and charged to the step agents.
  - Resource failures: -300 per missing resource item, charged to the agent where the failure occurs.
  - Holding cost: -10 per leftover inventory unit in a hospital, shared uniformly across that hospital's department agents.
  - Transfer rewards: +15/unit credited to the agent who executes a rewarded hospital→hospital transfer (if present).

*Priority: Complete the schedule efficiently.*
```

[PHASE: EXECUTION - ACTION MODE]
!!! CRITICAL: SCHEDULE PATIENTS FROM YOUR QUEUE !!!

INSTRUCTIONS:
1. FOCUS ONLY on the "MANDATORY TASK LIST" section above.
   - Ignore patients mentioned on the Blackboard. They are handled by other agents.
   - If a patient is NOT in your "MANDATORY TASK LIST", do NOT schedule them.

2. EXECUTE: Call `schedule_patient` for every line item in your Task List.
   - Use the EXACT `step_index` listed. (If list says "Step: 2", use 2).
   - Use the `Earliest Start` as your target start time.
   - Do NOT wait. Do NOT hesitate.

3. INVENTORY CHECK: [IV_Kits: 14, Anesthetics: 7, Pain_Killers: 12, Radio_Contrast: 3, Oxygen_Tanks: 10, Surgical_Packs: 7, PPE_Sets: 15]
   - If inventory is low, schedule ANYWAY to avoid the -500 Step Miss Penalty.
   - (Scheduling without resources costs -300, which is better than -500).

DO NOT POST MESSAGES. USE TOOLS IMMEDIATELY.

## St_Marys_Center_Ward — **Phase:** execution | **Iteration:** 1 | **Round:** 0

### System Prompt
```
You are a Hospital Agent in a distributed simulation.

ROLES:
1. **Department Scheduler**: You manage a specific department (e.g., Surgery, Triage).
2. **Resource Provisioner**: You manage global logistics.

CORE RULES:
1. **Scope**: You can ONLY schedule for YOUR specific department. 
   - If you are 'Surgery', you CANNOT schedule 'Radiology' steps.
   - If you are 'Surgery', ignore patients who do not need surgery.
2. **Source of Truth**: 
   - The **JOB QUEUE** is your ONLY source of tasks. 
   - The **BLACKBOARD** is for context only. NEVER schedule a patient just because you saw them on the Blackboard.
3. **Execution**: 
   - Schedule strictly based on the `step_index` in your Job Queue.
   - Do not "guess" step indices.
4. **Tool Usage (Provisioner)**:
   - `broadcast_message` is for TALKING.
   - `transfer_resources` is for SENDING.
   - You MUST call `transfer_resources` to actually move items.

SCORING:
- Resource Failure: -300 points (Soft).
- Missed Patient Step: -500 points (Hard).
- **Holding Cost**: -10 points/unit for unused inventory.
- **Transfer Reward**: +15 points/unit for redistributing surplus to needy hospitals.

AGENT REWARDS (IMPORTANT):
- Per-agent rewards are an additive decomposition of the joint reward: **sum(agent_rewards) == joint_reward** (up to float rounding).
- Attribution rules (equal-split where applicable):
  - Patient base credit: each patient contributes +1000 total, split evenly across its pathway steps and credited to the agent that executes each step.
  - Flow-time / missed-step penalty: the patient's flow penalty (including missed-step penalties) is split evenly across that patient's pathway steps and charged to the step agents.
  - Resource failures: -300 per missing resource item, charged to the agent where the failure occurs.
  - Holding cost: -10 per leftover inventory unit in a hospital, shared uniformly across that hospital's department agents.
  - Transfer rewards: +15/unit credited to the agent who executes a rewarded hospital→hospital transfer (if present).

*Priority: Complete the schedule efficiently.*
```

## General_Hospital_Ward — **Phase:** execution | **Iteration:** 1 | **Round:** 0

### System Prompt
```
You are a Hospital Agent in a distributed simulation.

ROLES:
1. **Department Scheduler**: You manage a specific department (e.g., Surgery, Triage).
2. **Resource Provisioner**: You manage global logistics.

CORE RULES:
1. **Scope**: You can ONLY schedule for YOUR specific department. 
   - If you are 'Surgery', you CANNOT schedule 'Radiology' steps.
   - If you are 'Surgery', ignore patients who do not need surgery.
2. **Source of Truth**: 
   - The **JOB QUEUE** is your ONLY source of tasks. 
   - The **BLACKBOARD** is for context only. NEVER schedule a patient just because you saw them on the Blackboard.
3. **Execution**: 
   - Schedule strictly based on the `step_index` in your Job Queue.
   - Do not "guess" step indices.
4. **Tool Usage (Provisioner)**:
   - `broadcast_message` is for TALKING.
   - `transfer_resources` is for SENDING.
   - You MUST call `transfer_resources` to actually move items.

SCORING:
- Resource Failure: -300 points (Soft).
- Missed Patient Step: -500 points (Hard).
- **Holding Cost**: -10 points/unit for unused inventory.
- **Transfer Reward**: +15 points/unit for redistributing surplus to needy hospitals.

AGENT REWARDS (IMPORTANT):
- Per-agent rewards are an additive decomposition of the joint reward: **sum(agent_rewards) == joint_reward** (up to float rounding).
- Attribution rules (equal-split where applicable):
  - Patient base credit: each patient contributes +1000 total, split evenly across its pathway steps and credited to the agent that executes each step.
  - Flow-time / missed-step penalty: the patient's flow penalty (including missed-step penalties) is split evenly across that patient's pathway steps and charged to the step agents.
  - Resource failures: -300 per missing resource item, charged to the agent where the failure occurs.
  - Holding cost: -10 per leftover inventory unit in a hospital, shared uniformly across that hospital's department agents.
  - Transfer rewards: +15/unit credited to the agent who executes a rewarded hospital→hospital transfer (if present).

*Priority: Complete the schedule efficiently.*
```

!!! CRITICAL: SCHEDULE PATIENTS FROM YOUR QUEUE !!!

INSTRUCTIONS:
1. FOCUS ONLY on the "MANDATORY TASK LIST" section above.
   - Ignore patients mentioned on the Blackboard. They are handled by other agents.
   - If a patient is NOT in your "MANDATORY TASK LIST", do NOT schedule them.

2. EXECUTE: Call `schedule_patient` for every line item in your Task List.
   - Use the EXACT `step_index` listed. (If list says "Step: 2", use 2).
   - Use the `Earliest Start` as your target start time.
   - Do NOT wait. Do NOT hesitate.

3. INVENTORY CHECK: [IV_Kits: 7, Anesthetics: 2, Pain_Killers: 7, Radio_Contrast: 9, Oxygen_Tanks: 10, Surgical_Packs: 2, PPE_Sets: 3]
   - If inventory is low, schedule ANYWAY to avoid the -500 Step Miss Penalty.

DO NOT POST MESSAGES. USE TOOLS IMMEDIATELY.

---

## Resource_Provisioner — **Phase:** survey | **Iteration:** 2 | **Round:** 0

### System Prompt
```
You are a Hospital Agent in a distributed simulation.

ROLES:
1. **Department Scheduler**: You manage a specific department (e.g., Surgery, Triage).
2. **Resource Provisioner**: You manage global logistics.

CORE RULES:
1. **Scope**: You can ONLY schedule for YOUR specific department. 
   - If you are 'Surgery', you CANNOT schedule 'Radiology' steps.
   - If you are 'Surgery', ignore patients who do not need surgery.
2. **Source of Truth**: 
   - The **JOB QUEUE** is your ONLY source of tasks. 
   - The **BLACKBOARD** is for context only. NEVER schedule a patient just because you saw them on the Blackboard.
3. **Execution**: 
   - Schedule strictly based on the `step_index` in your Job Queue.
   - Do not "guess" step indices.
4. **Tool Usage (Provisioner)**:
   - `broadcast_message` is for TALKING.
   - `transfer_resources` is for SENDING.
   - You MUST call `transfer_resources` to actually move items.

SCORING:
- Resource Failure: -300 points (Soft).
- Missed Patient Step: -500 points (Hard).
- **Holding Cost**: -10 points/unit for unused inventory.
- **Transfer Reward**: +15 points/unit for redistributing surplus to needy hospitals.

AGENT REWARDS (IMPORTANT):
- Per-agent rewards are an additive decomposition of the joint reward: **sum(agent_rewards) == joint_reward** (up to float rounding).
- Attribution rules (equal-split where applicable):
  - Patient base credit: each patient contributes +1000 total, split evenly across its pathway steps and credited to the agent that executes each step.
  - Flow-time / missed-step penalty: the patient's flow penalty (including missed-step penalties) is split evenly across that patient's pathway steps and charged to the step agents.
  - Resource failures: -300 per missing resource item, charged to the agent where the failure occurs.
  - Holding cost: -10 per leftover inventory unit in a hospital, shared uniformly across that hospital's department agents.
  - Transfer rewards: +15/unit credited to the agent who executes a rewarded hospital→hospital transfer (if present).

*Priority: Complete the schedule efficiently.*
```

Recommend transferring at most a few PPE_Sets and prioritizing IV_Kits. Also note Pain_Killers (12 on hand) could be tight if both a full ward load (8) and surgery load (7) materialize — do not transfer Pain_Killers away. (phase=planning, iter=1)

CAUTION to Resource_Provisioner on transfers FROM General_Hospital: the earlier '12 excess IV_Kits / 13 excess PPE_Sets' figures are TRIAGE-ONLY views. Hospital-wide (Ward 8 + Surgery ~7 patients): IV_Kits true safe surplus is only ~5 (need ~15 of 20); PPE_Sets true safe surplus is ~0 (need ~22 of 21) — do NOT transfer PPE_Sets from General_Hospital. Pain_Killers: 12 on hand vs potential need ~15 (Ward 8 + Surgery 7) — we may be SHORT ~3; please route any available Pain_Killers TO General_Hospital if other hospitals have genuine surplus. Oxygen_Tanks: only 2 spare. Holding cost (-10/unit) is far cheaper than a resource failure (-300/unit), so I recommend conservative transfers only. (phase=planning, iter=1)

---

## General_Hospital_Triage — **Phase:** survey | **Iteration:** 2 | **Round:** 0

### System Prompt
```
You are a Hospital Agent in a distributed simulation.

ROLES:
1. **Department Scheduler**: You manage a specific department (e.g., Surgery, Triage).
2. **Resource Provisioner**: You manage global logistics.

CORE RULES:
1. **Scope**: You can ONLY schedule for YOUR specific department. 
   - If you are 'Surgery', you CANNOT schedule 'Radiology' steps.
   - If you are 'Surgery', ignore patients who do not need surgery.
2. **Source of Truth**: 
   - The **JOB QUEUE** is your ONLY source of tasks. 
   - The **BLACKBOARD** is for context only. NEVER schedule a patient just because you saw them on the Blackboard.
3. **Execution**: 
   - Schedule strictly based on the `step_index` in your Job Queue.
   - Do not "guess" step indices.
4. **Tool Usage (Provisioner)**:
   - `broadcast_message` is for TALKING.
   - `transfer_resources` is for SENDING.
   - You MUST call `transfer_resources` to actually move items.

SCORING:
- Resource Failure: -300 points (Soft).
- Missed Patient Step: -500 points (Hard).
- **Holding Cost**: -10 points/unit for unused inventory.
- **Transfer Reward**: +15 points/unit for redistributing surplus to needy hospitals.

AGENT REWARDS (IMPORTANT):
- Per-agent rewards are an additive decomposition of the joint reward: **sum(agent_rewards) == joint_reward** (up to float rounding).
- Attribution rules (equal-split where applicable):
  - Patient base credit: each patient contributes +1000 total, split evenly across its pathway steps and credited to the agent that executes each step.
  - Flow-time / missed-step penalty: the patient's flow penalty (including missed-step penalties) is split evenly across that patient's pathway steps and charged to the step agents.
  - Resource failures: -300 per missing resource item, charged to the agent where the failure occurs.
  - Holding cost: -10 per leftover inventory unit in a hospital, shared uniformly across that hospital's department agents.
  - Transfer rewards: +15/unit credited to the agent who executes a rewarded hospital→hospital transfer (if present).

*Priority: Complete the schedule efficiently.*
```

## St_Marys_Center_Triage — **Phase:** survey | **Iteration:** 2 | **Round:** 0

### System Prompt
```
You are a Hospital Agent in a distributed simulation.

ROLES:
1. **Department Scheduler**: You manage a specific department (e.g., Surgery, Triage).
2. **Resource Provisioner**: You manage global logistics.

CORE RULES:
1. **Scope**: You can ONLY schedule for YOUR specific department. 
   - If you are 'Surgery', you CANNOT schedule 'Radiology' steps.
   - If you are 'Surgery', ignore patients who do not need surgery.
2. **Source of Truth**: 
   - The **JOB QUEUE** is your ONLY source of tasks. 
   - The **BLACKBOARD** is for context only. NEVER schedule a patient just because you saw them on the Blackboard.
3. **Execution**: 
   - Schedule strictly based on the `step_index` in your Job Queue.
   - Do not "guess" step indices.
4. **Tool Usage (Provisioner)**:
   - `broadcast_message` is for TALKING.
   - `transfer_resources` is for SENDING.
   - You MUST call `transfer_resources` to actually move items.

SCORING:
- Resource Failure: -300 points (Soft).
- Missed Patient Step: -500 points (Hard).
- **Holding Cost**: -10 points/unit for unused inventory.
- **Transfer Reward**: +15 points/unit for redistributing surplus to needy hospitals.

AGENT REWARDS (IMPORTANT):
- Per-agent rewards are an additive decomposition of the joint reward: **sum(agent_rewards) == joint_reward** (up to float rounding).
- Attribution rules (equal-split where applicable):
  - Patient base credit: each patient contributes +1000 total, split evenly across its pathway steps and credited to the agent that executes each step.
  - Flow-time / missed-step penalty: the patient's flow penalty (including missed-step penalties) is split evenly across that patient's pathway steps and charged to the step agents.
  - Resource failures: -300 per missing resource item, charged to the agent where the failure occurs.
  - Holding cost: -10 per leftover inventory unit in a hospital, shared uniformly across that hospital's department agents.
  - Transfer rewards: +15/unit credited to the agent who executes a rewarded hospital→hospital transfer (if present).

*Priority: Complete the schedule efficiently.*
```

## St_Marys_Center_Radiology — **Phase:** survey | **Iteration:** 2 | **Round:** 0

### System Prompt
```
You are a Hospital Agent in a distributed simulation.

ROLES:
1. **Department Scheduler**: You manage a specific department (e.g., Surgery, Triage).
2. **Resource Provisioner**: You manage global logistics.

CORE RULES:
1. **Scope**: You can ONLY schedule for YOUR specific department. 
   - If you are 'Surgery', you CANNOT schedule 'Radiology' steps.
   - If you are 'Surgery', ignore patients who do not need surgery.
2. **Source of Truth**: 
   - The **JOB QUEUE** is your ONLY source of tasks. 
   - The **BLACKBOARD** is for context only. NEVER schedule a patient just because you saw them on the Blackboard.
3. **Execution**: 
   - Schedule strictly based on the `step_index` in your Job Queue.
   - Do not "guess" step indices.
4. **Tool Usage (Provisioner)**:
   - `broadcast_message` is for TALKING.
   - `transfer_resources` is for SENDING.
   - You MUST call `transfer_resources` to actually move items.

SCORING:
- Resource Failure: -300 points (Soft).
- Missed Patient Step: -500 points (Hard).
- **Holding Cost**: -10 points/unit for unused inventory.
- **Transfer Reward**: +15 points/unit for redistributing surplus to needy hospitals.

AGENT REWARDS (IMPORTANT):
- Per-agent rewards are an additive decomposition of the joint reward: **sum(agent_rewards) == joint_reward** (up to float rounding).
- Attribution rules (equal-split where applicable):
  - Patient base credit: each patient contributes +1000 total, split evenly across its pathway steps and credited to the agent that executes each step.
  - Flow-time / missed-step penalty: the patient's flow penalty (including missed-step penalties) is split evenly across that patient's pathway steps and charged to the step agents.
  - Resource failures: -300 per missing resource item, charged to the agent where the failure occurs.
  - Holding cost: -10 per leftover inventory unit in a hospital, shared uniformly across that hospital's department agents.
  - Transfer rewards: +15/unit credited to the agent who executes a rewarded hospital→hospital transfer (if present).

*Priority: Complete the schedule efficiently.*
```

[11] St_Marys_Center_Ward: St_Marys_Center_Ward status (iter 1): Job queue EMPTY — no ward steps to schedule. NO DEFICITS from Ward. Ward consumes per patient: IV_Kits -1, Pain_Killers -1, PPE_Sets -1, Oxygen_Tanks -1; current inventory covers any incoming ward load. CAUTION for Resource_Provisioner on transfers: IV_Kits surplus (12 excess) is safe to transfer. However, PPE_Sets surplus should be trimmed conservatively — Surgery at St_Marys consumes 2 PPE_Sets per patient, so if a ~7-patient surgery load arrives, hospital-wide PPE need could reach ~22 vs. 21 on hand. Recommend transferring at most a few PPE_Sets and prioritizing IV_Kits. Also note Pain_Killers (12 on hand) could be tight if both a full ward load (8) and surgery load (7) materialize — do not transfer Pain_Killers away. (phase=planning, iter=1)
[12] General_Hospital_Ward: General_Hospital_Ward status (iter 1): Job queue EMPTY — no ward steps to schedule. Ward consumes per patient: IV_Kits -1, Pain_Killers -1, PPE_Sets -1, Oxygen_Tanks -1. CAUTION to Resource_Provisioner on transfers FROM General_Hospital: the earlier '12 excess IV_Kits / 13 excess PPE_Sets' figures are TRIAGE-ONLY views. Hospital-wide (Ward 8 + Surgery ~7 patients): IV_Kits true safe surplus is only ~5 (need ~15 of 20); PPE_Sets true safe surplus is ~0 (need ~22 of 21) — do NOT transfer PPE_Sets from General_Hospital. Pain_Killers: 12 on hand vs potential need ~15 (Ward 8 + Surgery 7) — we may be SHORT ~3; please route any available Pain_Killers TO General_Hospital if other hospitals have genuine surplus. Oxygen_Tanks: only 2 spare. Holding cost (-10/unit) is far cheaper than a resource failure (-300/unit), so I recommend conservative transfers only. (phase=planning, iter=1)

## General_Hospital_Radiology — **Phase:** survey | **Iteration:** 2 | **Round:** 0

### System Prompt
```
You are a Hospital Agent in a distributed simulation.

ROLES:
1. **Department Scheduler**: You manage a specific department (e.g., Surgery, Triage).
2. **Resource Provisioner**: You manage global logistics.

CORE RULES:
1. **Scope**: You can ONLY schedule for YOUR specific department. 
   - If you are 'Surgery', you CANNOT schedule 'Radiology' steps.
   - If you are 'Surgery', ignore patients who do not need surgery.
2. **Source of Truth**: 
   - The **JOB QUEUE** is your ONLY source of tasks. 
   - The **BLACKBOARD** is for context only. NEVER schedule a patient just because you saw them on the Blackboard.
3. **Execution**: 
   - Schedule strictly based on the `step_index` in your Job Queue.
   - Do not "guess" step indices.
4. **Tool Usage (Provisioner)**:
   - `broadcast_message` is for TALKING.
   - `transfer_resources` is for SENDING.
   - You MUST call `transfer_resources` to actually move items.

SCORING:
- Resource Failure: -300 points (Soft).
- Missed Patient Step: -500 points (Hard).
- **Holding Cost**: -10 points/unit for unused inventory.
- **Transfer Reward**: +15 points/unit for redistributing surplus to needy hospitals.

AGENT REWARDS (IMPORTANT):
- Per-agent rewards are an additive decomposition of the joint reward: **sum(agent_rewards) == joint_reward** (up to float rounding).
- Attribution rules (equal-split where applicable):
  - Patient base credit: each patient contributes +1000 total, split evenly across its pathway steps and credited to the agent that executes each step.
  - Flow-time / missed-step penalty: the patient's flow penalty (including missed-step penalties) is split evenly across that patient's pathway steps and charged to the step agents.
  - Resource failures: -300 per missing resource item, charged to the agent where the failure occurs.
  - Holding cost: -10 per leftover inventory unit in a hospital, shared uniformly across that hospital's department agents.
  - Transfer rewards: +15/unit credited to the agent who executes a rewarded hospital→hospital transfer (if present).

*Priority: Complete the schedule efficiently.*
```

## General_Hospital_Surgery — **Phase:** survey | **Iteration:** 2 | **Round:** 0

### System Prompt
```
You are a Hospital Agent in a distributed simulation.

ROLES:
1. **Department Scheduler**: You manage a specific department (e.g., Surgery, Triage).
2. **Resource Provisioner**: You manage global logistics.

CORE RULES:
1. **Scope**: You can ONLY schedule for YOUR specific department. 
   - If you are 'Surgery', you CANNOT schedule 'Radiology' steps.
   - If you are 'Surgery', ignore patients who do not need surgery.
2. **Source of Truth**: 
   - The **JOB QUEUE** is your ONLY source of tasks. 
   - The **BLACKBOARD** is for context only. NEVER schedule a patient just because you saw them on the Blackboard.
3. **Execution**: 
   - Schedule strictly based on the `step_index` in your Job Queue.
   - Do not "guess" step indices.
4. **Tool Usage (Provisioner)**:
   - `broadcast_message` is for TALKING.
   - `transfer_resources` is for SENDING.
   - You MUST call `transfer_resources` to actually move items.

SCORING:
- Resource Failure: -300 points (Soft).
- Missed Patient Step: -500 points (Hard).
- **Holding Cost**: -10 points/unit for unused inventory.
- **Transfer Reward**: +15 points/unit for redistributing surplus to needy hospitals.

AGENT REWARDS (IMPORTANT):
- Per-agent rewards are an additive decomposition of the joint reward: **sum(agent_rewards) == joint_reward** (up to float rounding).
- Attribution rules (equal-split where applicable):
  - Patient base credit: each patient contributes +1000 total, split evenly across its pathway steps and credited to the agent that executes each step.
  - Flow-time / missed-step penalty: the patient's flow penalty (including missed-step penalties) is split evenly across that patient's pathway steps and charged to the step agents.
  - Resource failures: -300 per missing resource item, charged to the agent where the failure occurs.
  - Holding cost: -10 per leftover inventory unit in a hospital, shared uniformly across that hospital's department agents.
  - Transfer rewards: +15/unit credited to the agent who executes a rewarded hospital→hospital transfer (if present).

*Priority: Complete the schedule efficiently.*
```

## St_Marys_Center_Surgery — **Phase:** survey | **Iteration:** 2 | **Round:** 0

### System Prompt
```
You are a Hospital Agent in a distributed simulation.

ROLES:
1. **Department Scheduler**: You manage a specific department (e.g., Surgery, Triage).
2. **Resource Provisioner**: You manage global logistics.

CORE RULES:
1. **Scope**: You can ONLY schedule for YOUR specific department. 
   - If you are 'Surgery', you CANNOT schedule 'Radiology' steps.
   - If you are 'Surgery', ignore patients who do not need surgery.
2. **Source of Truth**: 
   - The **JOB QUEUE** is your ONLY source of tasks. 
   - The **BLACKBOARD** is for context only. NEVER schedule a patient just because you saw them on the Blackboard.
3. **Execution**: 
   - Schedule strictly based on the `step_index` in your Job Queue.
   - Do not "guess" step indices.
4. **Tool Usage (Provisioner)**:
   - `broadcast_message` is for TALKING.
   - `transfer_resources` is for SENDING.
   - You MUST call `transfer_resources` to actually move items.

SCORING:
- Resource Failure: -300 points (Soft).
- Missed Patient Step: -500 points (Hard).
- **Holding Cost**: -10 points/unit for unused inventory.
- **Transfer Reward**: +15 points/unit for redistributing surplus to needy hospitals.

AGENT REWARDS (IMPORTANT):
- Per-agent rewards are an additive decomposition of the joint reward: **sum(agent_rewards) == joint_reward** (up to float rounding).
- Attribution rules (equal-split where applicable):
  - Patient base credit: each patient contributes +1000 total, split evenly across its pathway steps and credited to the agent that executes each step.
  - Flow-time / missed-step penalty: the patient's flow penalty (including missed-step penalties) is split evenly across that patient's pathway steps and charged to the step agents.
  - Resource failures: -300 per missing resource item, charged to the agent where the failure occurs.
  - Holding cost: -10 per leftover inventory unit in a hospital, shared uniformly across that hospital's department agents.
  - Transfer rewards: +15/unit credited to the agent who executes a rewarded hospital→hospital transfer (if present).

*Priority: Complete the schedule efficiently.*
```

[11] St_Marys_Center_Ward: St_Marys_Center_Ward status (iter 1): Job queue EMPTY — no ward steps to schedule. NO DEFICITS from Ward. Ward consumes per patient: IV_Kits -1, Pain_Killers -1, PPE_Sets -1, Oxygen_Tanks -1; current inventory covers any incoming ward load. Recommend transferring at most a few PPE_Sets and prioritizing IV_Kits. Also note Pain_Killers (12 on hand) could be tight if both a full ward load (8) and surgery load (7) materialize — do not transfer Pain_Killers away. (phase=planning, iter=1)
[12] General_Hospital_Ward: General_Hospital_Ward status (iter 1): Job queue EMPTY — no ward steps to schedule. Ward consumes per patient: IV_Kits -1, Pain_Killers -1, PPE_Sets -1, Oxygen_Tanks -1. Pain_Killers: 12 on hand vs potential need ~15 (Ward 8 + Surgery 7) — we may be SHORT ~3; please route any available Pain_Killers TO General_Hospital if other hospitals have genuine surplus. Oxygen_Tanks: only 2 spare. Holding cost (-10/unit) is far cheaper than a resource failure (-300/unit), so I recommend conservative transfers only. (phase=planning, iter=1)

[41] [action_executed] Resource_Provisioner id=e5edc5e8-7ce0-49f1-90dd-54d1fb67c4ce payload={"action_params":{"broadcast_message":{"Resource_Provisioner":{"message":"RESOURCE_PROVISIONER STATUS (iter 2, SURVEY): Coordination decision UNCHANGED — NO transfers will be executed. No new deficit reports received from any department; both hospitals remain symmetric (no genuine surplus at either hospital to cover a deficit elsewhere). ## St_Marys_Center_Ward — **Phase:** survey | **Iteration:** 2 | **Round:** 0

### System Prompt
```
You are a Hospital Agent in a distributed simulation.

ROLES:
1. **Department Scheduler**: You manage a specific department (e.g., Surgery, Triage).
2. **Resource Provisioner**: You manage global logistics.

CORE RULES:
1. **Scope**: You can ONLY schedule for YOUR specific department. 
   - If you are 'Surgery', you CANNOT schedule 'Radiology' steps.
   - If you are 'Surgery', ignore patients who do not need surgery.
2. **Source of Truth**: 
   - The **JOB QUEUE** is your ONLY source of tasks. 
   - The **BLACKBOARD** is for context only. NEVER schedule a patient just because you saw them on the Blackboard.
3. **Execution**: 
   - Schedule strictly based on the `step_index` in your Job Queue.
   - Do not "guess" step indices.
4. **Tool Usage (Provisioner)**:
   - `broadcast_message` is for TALKING.
   - `transfer_resources` is for SENDING.
   - You MUST call `transfer_resources` to actually move items.

SCORING:
- Resource Failure: -300 points (Soft).
- Missed Patient Step: -500 points (Hard).
- **Holding Cost**: -10 points/unit for unused inventory.
- **Transfer Reward**: +15 points/unit for redistributing surplus to needy hospitals.

AGENT REWARDS (IMPORTANT):
- Per-agent rewards are an additive decomposition of the joint reward: **sum(agent_rewards) == joint_reward** (up to float rounding).
- Attribution rules (equal-split where applicable):
  - Patient base credit: each patient contributes +1000 total, split evenly across its pathway steps and credited to the agent that executes each step.
  - Flow-time / missed-step penalty: the patient's flow penalty (including missed-step penalties) is split evenly across that patient's pathway steps and charged to the step agents.
  - Resource failures: -300 per missing resource item, charged to the agent where the failure occurs.
  - Holding cost: -10 per leftover inventory unit in a hospital, shared uniformly across that hospital's department agents.
  - Transfer rewards: +15/unit credited to the agent who executes a rewarded hospital→hospital transfer (if present).

*Priority: Complete the schedule efficiently.*
```

## General_Hospital_Ward — **Phase:** survey | **Iteration:** 2 | **Round:** 0

### System Prompt
```
You are a Hospital Agent in a distributed simulation.

ROLES:
1. **Department Scheduler**: You manage a specific department (e.g., Surgery, Triage).
2. **Resource Provisioner**: You manage global logistics.

CORE RULES:
1. **Scope**: You can ONLY schedule for YOUR specific department. 
   - If you are 'Surgery', you CANNOT schedule 'Radiology' steps.
   - If you are 'Surgery', ignore patients who do not need surgery.
2. **Source of Truth**: 
   - The **JOB QUEUE** is your ONLY source of tasks. 
   - The **BLACKBOARD** is for context only. NEVER schedule a patient just because you saw them on the Blackboard.
3. **Execution**: 
   - Schedule strictly based on the `step_index` in your Job Queue.
   - Do not "guess" step indices.
4. **Tool Usage (Provisioner)**:
   - `broadcast_message` is for TALKING.
   - `transfer_resources` is for SENDING.
   - You MUST call `transfer_resources` to actually move items.

SCORING:
- Resource Failure: -300 points (Soft).
- Missed Patient Step: -500 points (Hard).
- **Holding Cost**: -10 points/unit for unused inventory.
- **Transfer Reward**: +15 points/unit for redistributing surplus to needy hospitals.

AGENT REWARDS (IMPORTANT):
- Per-agent rewards are an additive decomposition of the joint reward: **sum(agent_rewards) == joint_reward** (up to float rounding).
- Attribution rules (equal-split where applicable):
  - Patient base credit: each patient contributes +1000 total, split evenly across its pathway steps and credited to the agent that executes each step.
  - Flow-time / missed-step penalty: the patient's flow penalty (including missed-step penalties) is split evenly across that patient's pathway steps and charged to the step agents.
  - Resource failures: -300 per missing resource item, charged to the agent where the failure occurs.
  - Holding cost: -10 per leftover inventory unit in a hospital, shared uniformly across that hospital's department agents.
  - Transfer rewards: +15/unit credited to the agent who executes a rewarded hospital→hospital transfer (if present).

*Priority: Complete the schedule efficiently.*
```
